<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every finite <i>N</i> ≥ 2, we prove that undeformed $`\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}\nolimits (N)`$ BFSS matrix quantum mechanics has exactly one normalizable zero-energy state after removing the center of mass. This establishes the threshold-bound-state conjecture.

- **claim-main-kernel-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $N\ge2$, $$\dim\mathop{\mathrm{ker}}H_N=1.$$ The kernel consists of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(9)$-invariant states and is even in the fermion grading just specified.
  - Explanation: For every matrix size at least two, the relative undeformed SU(N) Hamiltonian has exactly one normalizable zero-energy state. That state is invariant under Spin(9) and even in the fermion grading.
- **claim-square-of-charge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-square)): On the gauge-invariant core, $$\label{eq:square} \begin{aligned} \mathcal H(h,m)&=\frac12p^2+U_{h,m} +\frac{\mathrm i}{2}\theta^t\left(\frac32mT-hK_j\gamma^j\right)\theta, \qquad K_j=\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}(x_j),\\ 2U_{h,m}&=\sum_{\mathrm{cyc}} \left\lvert h[x_a,x_b]-2mx_c\right\rvert^2 +h^2\sum_{\substack{i<j\\j>3}}\left\lvert [x_i,x_j]\right\rvert^2 +m^2\sum_{d=4}^9\left\lvert x_d\right\rvert^2,\\ D_v(h,m)^2&=\mathcal H(h,m)+mJ(R(v)), \qquad R(v)_{ij}=s_jv^t\gamma^i\gamma^jTv. \end{aligned}$$ The cyclic sum uses $(a,b,c)=(1,2,3),(2,3,1),(3,1,2)$. The matrix $R(v)$ is skew, preserves $\mathbb R^3\oplus\mathbb R^6$, and has zero spin-basis average. The same formulas hold for an orthogonal direct sum of compact color algebras and abelian color spaces.
  - Explanation: Gives the exact square of one deformed supercharge on gauge-invariant functions. It splits into the averaged form energy, a confining potential, a fermion bilinear and a rotation-generator term.
- **claim-coercivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-coercive-charge)): On gauge- and $G$-invariant vectors, in the sense of quadratic forms, $$\label{eq:coercive-charge} \left\lVert D_u(h,m)\psi\right\rVert^2\geq\frac23\mathcal H(h,m)[\psi].$$
  - Explanation: On vectors invariant under gauge transformations and the fixing torus, the squared norm of the single charge along the chosen spinor controls a fixed fraction of the averaged form energy.
- **claim-domains** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-domains)): For fixed $h,m,v$, the charge $D_v(h,m)$ is essentially self-adjoint on the full smooth compactly supported core. Its closed domain is its distributional maximal domain $$\{\psi\in L^2:D_v(h,m)\psi\in L^2\text{ in distributions}\}.$$ The same statement holds after restriction to any commuting compact group of gauge or rotation symmetries. On gauge invariants at $m=0$, all unit-spinor charges have the same graph domain, and $$\left\lVert D_v(h,0)\psi\right\rVert^2=\mathcal H(h,0)[\psi].$$ Consequently the distributional $L^2$ kernel of any one of these charges is their common kernel. At $h=1$ it is exactly $\mathop{\mathrm{ker}}H_N$ for the form Hamiltonian in <a href="#eq:model-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:model-form">[eq:model-form]</a>.
  - Explanation: Each charge is essentially self-adjoint on smooth compactly supported functions. Its closed domain is the distributional maximal domain. At zero mass all unit-spinor charges share one graph domain and one kernel, which at unit coupling is the kernel of the Hamiltonian.
- **claim-rotation-anticommutator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-rotation-exact)): For any orthonormal real spin basis, $$\label{alg:rotation-exact-eq} \sum_{\nu=1}^{16}\{D_{v_\nu}(h,0),M_{v_\nu}(F)\}=J(F)$$ on the smooth compactly supported core.
  - Explanation: An exact identity expresses every rotation generator as a sum of anticommutators of the undeformed charges with explicit linear spin-position operators.
- **claim-singlet-parity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-singlet-parity)): Every gauge-invariant $L^2$ vector in the undeformed kernel is $\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(9)$-invariant and even. In particular it is $G$-invariant. This assertion does not require finite-dimensionality of the kernel or any moment of its vectors.
  - Explanation: Every gauge-invariant square-integrable zero-energy vector is a Spin(9) singlet and even. No finite-dimensionality or moment assumption is used.
- **claim-massive-index** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-massive-index)): For every $h>0$, the graph domain of $D_h$ embeds compactly into $\mathscr H_G$, and its even-to-odd part is Fredholm. Its index is $$\label{eq:massive-index} \operatorname{ind}_G D_u(h,1) :=\dim\mathop{\mathrm{ker}}(D_h\|_{\mathscr H_G^+}) -\dim\mathop{\mathrm{ker}}(D_h\|_{\mathscr H_G^-})=p(N),$$ where $p(N)$ is the number of unordered integer partitions of $N$. For all sufficiently small $h>0$, the massive kernel itself has dimension $p(N)$, consists of even vectors, and is separated from the rest of the spectrum of $D_h$ by a positive gap uniform in $h$.
  - Explanation: The massive charge has compact resolvent and a Fredholm even-to-odd part whose graded index equals the partition number of N for every positive coupling. At small coupling the kernel is even, of that dimension, and uniformly gapped.
- **claim-massive-potential** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-massive-potential)): There are $c>0$ and $R<\infty$, depending only on $N$, such that $$\label{mass:potential-infinity} U_{1,1}(x)\geq c\left\lvert x\right\rvert^2\qquad(\left\lvert x\right\rvert\geq R).$$ Moreover, for $h,m>0$, $$\label{mass:potential-scaling} U_{h,m}(x)=\frac{m^4}{h^2} U_{1,1}\left(\frac hm x\right).$$
  - Explanation: The massive bosonic potential grows at least quadratically at large radius, and it obeys an exact scaling law in the coupling and mass.
- **claim-common-graph-domain** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mass-common-domain)): The domain of $D_h$, for every $h>0$, is $$\label{mass:graph-domain} \mathcal D= \left\{\psi\in\mathscr H_G\cap H^1: \left\lvert x\right\rvert\psi\in L^2,\quad [x_i,x_j]\psi\in L^2\ (i<j)\right\}.$$ On each compact interval $I\Subset(0,\infty)$, its graph norm is uniformly equivalent to $$\label{mass:graph-norm} \left\lVert \psi\right\rVert+\left\lVert \nabla\psi\right\rVert+\left\lVert \left\lvert x\right\rvert\psi\right\rVert +\sum_{i<j}\left\lVert [x_i,x_j]\psi\right\rVert.$$ The embedding $\mathcal D\hookrightarrow\mathscr H_G$ is compact, and $h\mapsto D_h$ is norm-resolvent continuous on $(0,\infty)$. Its graded Fredholm index is constant there.
  - Explanation: All massive charges share one graph domain described by first-derivative, position and bracket weights. The embedding into the invariant space is compact, the family is norm-resolvent continuous, and the graded index is constant in the coupling.
- **claim-normal-oscillators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-normal-oscillators)): The quadratic form $U_q^{(2)}$ is positive definite on $S_q$. The oscillator $H_q^{\mathrm{nor}}$ is nonnegative, has a unique zero-energy line, and has a positive spectral gap on its orthogonal complement. Its zero-energy line is even and is fixed by the entire stabilizer $H_q$, including exchanges of identical irreducible summands. It is also fixed by the combined first-three spatial and compensating gauge rotations, and by $\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(6)$.
  - Explanation: At each classical zero orbit the normal Hessian is positive definite, and the normal oscillator has a unique even zero-energy line with a gap. That line is fixed by the stabilizer and by the remaining rotations.
- **claim-distance-confinement** (bound, [source](#stmt-mass-distance-confinement)): For $0<h\leq1$, let $d_h(x)=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,h^{-1}Z)$. There are constants $c,C>0$, independent of $h$, such that $$\label{mass:distance-bound} U_{h,1}(x)\geq c\,d_h(x)^2, \qquad \mathcal H(h,1)[\psi]\geq \frac12\left\lVert \nabla\psi\right\rVert^2 +\frac c2\left\lVert d_h\psi\right\rVert^2-C\left\lVert \psi\right\rVert^2.$$ In particular, bounded form-energy families with bounded norm have uniform kinetic bounds and $\left\lVert \mathbf 1_{\{d_h>R\}}\psi\right\rVert^2\leq C'/R^2$.
  - Explanation: For small coupling the potential and the form energy are bounded below by the squared distance to the rescaled zero orbits. This gives uniform kinetic and tail control.
- **claim-slice-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mass-slice-compactness)): Suppose $h_n\downarrow0$ and $\psi_n$ are gauge-invariant vectors of bounded norm and bounded $\mathcal H(h_n,1)$ form energy. After a subsequence, there is one profile $f_q\in L^2(S_q)\otimes\mathcal F_N$ for each of the $p(N)$ zero orbits, with the following properties. Each profile is $H_q$-equivariant and belongs to the oscillator form domain. With the normalizations described in the proof, $$\label{mass:slice-exhaustion} \lim_{n\to\infty}\left\lVert \psi_n\right\rVert^2 =\sum_q\left\lVert f_q\right\rVert^2, \qquad \sum_q H_q^{\mathrm{nor}}[f_q] \leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathcal H(h_n,1)[\psi_n],$$ whenever the subsequence realizes the norm limit and energy liminf. For any fixed finite family of such sequences, extraction can be simultaneous and the norm identity polarizes to their inner products. Parity is preserved.
  - Explanation: Sequences with bounded norm and energy at vanishing coupling have subsequences with one equivariant profile per zero orbit. The profiles exhaust the norm and bound the energy from below, and extraction is simultaneous for finite lists.
- **claim-vacuum-recovery** (construction, [source](#stmt-mass-vacuum-recovery)): For every zero orbit there is a family $\Phi_{q,h}\in\mathcal D$ of even gauge- and $\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(3)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(6)$-invariant vectors such that, as $h\downarrow0$, $$\label{mass:vacuum-recovery-eq} \left\langle \Phi_{q,h},\Phi_{q',h}\right\rangle\longrightarrow\delta_{qq'}, \qquad \left\lVert D_h\Phi_{q,h}\right\rVert\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Trial states built from cut-off normal ground vectors on the tubes around each zero orbit are asymptotically orthonormal and have charge norm tending to zero as the coupling vanishes.
- **claim-raw-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-raw-estimates)): In either regime, for states supported in a fixed compact set, $$\\|\nabla\psi_h\\|\leq C\sqrt h, \qquad \sum_{i<j}\\|[x_i,x_j]\psi_h\\|\leq C h^{-1/2}. \label{eq:raw-estimates}$$ Consequently every weak limit of the squared norm measures on compact sets is supported on commuting configurations.
  - Explanation: Raw gradient and commutator estimates on compact sets show that every weak limit of the norm measures lives on commuting configurations.
- **claim-exact-slice** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slice)): In a neighborhood of the chosen configuration, gauge-equivariant functions are equivalent to $K_0$-equivariant functions on $$x=b+z,\qquad b\in\mathfrak k_0^9, \qquad z\in e^\perp\otimes\mathfrak n.$$ The block spectra of $b_e$ lie in fixed disjoint windows. Up to a fixed positive orbit-volume constant, the density on the slice is $$j(b)=\|\det_{\mathfrak n}K_e(b)\|, \qquad K_e(b)=\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}(b_e)\|_{\mathfrak n}. \label{fast:density}$$ It is independent of $z$. The exact normalization is as follows. Give $\mathop{\mathrm{U}}(N)/K_0$ the invariant volume induced by the trace metric on the off-block color space, let $V_C$ be its total volume, and use normalized Haar measure when integrating the gauge orbit. With fixed labeled spectral windows the integrated slice measure is $V_Cj(b)\,\mathrm db\,\mathrm dz$. Thus, with $d_f=8\dim\mathfrak n$, the unitary flat-measure pullback is $$\psi(b,t)=V_C^{1/2}j(b)^{1/2}h^{-d_f/4} \Psi(b+t/\sqrt h). \label{fast:unitary-normalization}$$ Here the original field is restricted to the slice on the right. A fixed labeled chart has no additional permutation multiplicity. When all labels are used, division by the number of permutations of equal-sized blocks gives the quotient measure used in Definition <a href="#def:profile-space" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:profile-space">19</a>. The same positive normalization is used in the inverse pullback for every test state. In these coordinates, $$D_v(h,m)=\sqrt h\,E_v(b)+D_v^s(h,m)+R_v(h,m). \label{eq:slice-charge}$$ Here $D_v^s$ is precisely the charge on $\mathfrak k_0^9$, including all its $h[b_i,b_j]$ multipliers. The operator $E_v(b)$ is an odd linear oscillator in $(p_t,t)$ using only fast fermions, with smooth coefficients independent of $h,m$. On a smaller chart the raw estimates imply $$\\|h^{-1/2}p_b\psi_h\\|+\\|p_t\psi_h\\|+\\|t\psi_h\\|\leq C. \label{fast:chart-raw}$$
  - Explanation: Near a commuting configuration with distinct centers, gauge-equivariant functions become equivariant functions of block and off-block variables. An explicit slice density appears, and the charge splits into a fast oscillator part, a slow block charge and a remainder.
- **claim-fast-line** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fast-line)): Let $c$ range in a compact set of scalar block configurations with distinct centers and with the chosen $e$-separations bounded below. On the neutral space, $E_v(c)$ has a one-dimensional even kernel, independent of $v$, and a gap bounded below uniformly on this compact set and on unit spinors $v$. For $b$ sufficiently close to that set, including noncommuting $b$, it has an even rank-one kernel projection $P_v(b)$ and a uniformly bounded inverse on $\mathop{\mathrm{ran}}(1-P_v(b))$.* *Let $\mathcal W^s$ be the oscillator Sobolev spaces defined below. For every prescribed finite integer $L$, the neighborhood can be chosen so that the parameter derivatives of $P_v$ and a normalized section $\chi_v(b)$ through order $L$ have bounded oscillator moments through order $L$. The reduced inverse, extended by zero on the kernel, and its parameter derivatives through order $L$ are uniformly bounded as maps $\mathcal W^s\to\mathcal W^{s+1}$ for $0\leq s\leq L$. The section can be chosen $K_0$-equivariantly so that its Berry form vanishes at $b=c$ in every slow direction. At scalar centers these sections are compatible with block permutations and the $\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(3)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(6)$ action, with no additional scalar character.
  - Explanation: On the neutral space the fast charge has an even one-dimensional kernel with a uniform gap, persisting with regular projection and bounded reduced inverse for nearby noncommuting blocks. A compatible equivariant phase section is constructed.
- **claim-fast-projection** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fast-projection)): In a compact subchart of Lemma <a href="#lem:fast-line" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:fast-line">16</a>, in either of the two local regimes, $$\\|(1-P_v)\psi_h\\|\longrightarrow0, \qquad \\|D_v^s(h,m)P_v\psi_h\\| \leq \begin{cases} O(1),&m\text{ fixed},\\ o(m),&\text{in \eqref{fast:growing-mass}}. \end{cases} \label{eq:fast-projection}$$ The second norm is on the full $(b,t)$ space. In the fixed-mass case, writing $P_v\psi_h=\chi_v(b)\eta_h(b)$ gives a bounded graph norm for $D_v^s(h,0)\eta_h$, where $\eta_h$ has the ordinary slow $K_0$-equivariance.
  - Explanation: In both local regimes the states concentrate on the fast line, and the slow charge of the projected state has the stated graph bound.
- **claim-diagonal-compression** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-diagonal-compression)): For fixed $m$, identify the range of $P_v(b)$ with the slow Clifford fiber by $a\mapsto\chi_v(b)a$. At $b=c$, the diagonal compression of <a href="#eq:slice-charge" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slice-charge">[eq:slice-charge]</a> has exactly the slow derivative symbol and the slow order-zero terms of $D_v^s(h,m)$, apart from terms whose coefficients tend to zero as $h\to\infty$. More precisely, after discarding explicitly $h^{-1/2}$-small coefficients, all additional order-zero coefficients are smooth and vanish at $b=c$.
  - Explanation: Compressing the full charge to the fast line at scalar blocks gives the slow charge, because gauge-spin terms cancel the derivative of the slice density.
- **claim-test-lifts** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-test-lifts)): Fix $m\geq0$, a unit spinor $v$, and a partition $C$ with $k\geq2$. For every $f\in\mathcal T_C$ there are gauge-singlet graph-domain states $I_{h,v,m}f$, defined for all sufficiently large $h$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \\|I_{h,v,m}f-\mathcal J_hf\\|&\longrightarrow0,\notag\\ \\|D_v(h,m)I_{h,v,m}f- \mathcal J_h(D_v^C(m)f)\\|&\longrightarrow0. \label{lift:graph-convergence} \end{aligned}$$ The construction is linear on any fixed finite-dimensional space of tests, and both conclusions hold simultaneously for every fixed finite test family. In particular, all inner products of their leading states and indicated charge outputs converge to the corresponding profile inner products. For distinct partitions the families, including their outputs, are asymptotically orthogonal. The leading identifications intertwine block permutations and $\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(3)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(6)$; each fixed symmetry may be passed through these norm limits. The lifts may depend on $v$ and $m$.
  - Explanation: Every profile test has a corrected lift in the graph domain whose norm and charge output converge to the profile test and its center charge. Lifts are linear on finite families and asymptotically orthogonal for distinct partitions.
- **claim-chart-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chart-compatibility)): On overlaps of separated-center charts, the identifications $\mathcal J_h$ agree to $o(1)$ in norm for each fixed test, with the natural center and internal fiber transports. The same is true for the indicated outputs $\mathcal J_hD_v^C(m)f$. A change of separating axis shifts the block variable $b$ by $O(\|z\|^2)$; its leading fast coordinate change is projection along the central conjugation directions, with the positive unitary density factor. The statements hold for finite test families, block relabelings, and each fixed spatial symmetry in $\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(3)\times\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(6)$.
  - Explanation: The local identifications on overlapping separated-center charts agree up to a vanishing norm error, including for the charge outputs and the natural symmetries.
- **claim-exterior-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-exterior-estimate)): For each $n\ge2$ there are constants $c_n>0$ and $R_n<\infty$ such that $$\label{eq:exterior-estimate} \left\lVert rD_u(h,0)F\right\rVert\ge c_n\left\lVert F\right\rVert, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}F\subset\{h^{1/3}r>R_n\}, \qquad r=\|x\|,$$ for every $h>0$ and every compactly supported gauge-invariant graph-domain vector for the $\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(n)$ operator. No rotation invariance is required.
  - Explanation: A weighted lower bound on the charge holds for vectors supported far out in the rescaled radius, with no rotation restriction. It is proved simultaneously with the kernel theorem.
- **claim-intermediate-scales** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-intermediate-scales)): Suppose <a href="#eq:exterior-estimate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exterior-estimate">[eq:exterior-estimate]</a> holds at size $n$. Let $F_h$ have the gauge symmetry of that block, with $\left\lVert F_h\right\rVert\le B$ and $\left\lVert D_u(h,0)F_h\right\rVert\le K$. Both norms may include arbitrary spectator variables or a spectator Hilbert space. There is an absolute cutoff constant $C$ such that, whenever $L\ge1$ and $M>e^LR_n$, $$\label{prof:intermediate-bound} \left(\int_{Mh^{-1/3}<r<\varepsilon}\|F_h\|^2\right)^{1/2} \le c_n^{-1}\left(e^L\varepsilon K+\frac{CB}{L}\right).$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:intermediate-scales} \lim_{\substack{\varepsilon\downarrow0\\M\to\infty}} \limsup_{h\to\infty} \int_{Mh^{-1/3}<r<\varepsilon}\|F_h\|^2=0.$$ The outer limit is directed: after choosing an error tolerance, one may choose $M_0$ and $\varepsilon_0$ so that the bound holds for every $M\ge M_0$ and $0<\varepsilon\le\varepsilon_0$.
  - Explanation: Given the exterior estimate at one size, the norm in a window between the internal scale and a small physical radius is bounded quantitatively and tends to zero in the directed limit.
- **claim-profile-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-profile-compactness)): Assume the induction hypotheses at all sizes less than $N$. Fix $m\ge0$, let $h_j\to\infty$, and suppose that $\psi_j$ is a bounded sequence of gauge-invariant vectors with locally bounded $D_u(h_j,m)$ graph norms. Here local boundedness means that $\left\lVert D_u(h_j,m)(a\psi_j)\right\rVert$ is bounded for every smooth compactly supported gauge-invariant multiplier $a$. After a subsequence there is a profile $F_C\in\mathcal P_C$ on each proper stratum such that the limiting norm measure restricted to that stratum is exactly its squared norm density. Moreover, for every test $f\in\mathcal T_C$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:test-lifts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:test-lifts">20</a>, $$\label{prof:test-overlap} \left\langle \psi_j,I_{h_j,u,m}f\right\rangle\longrightarrow\left\langle F_C,f\right\rangle.$$ The extraction can be made simultaneously for any finite list of sequences, and then the analogous restrictions of all their polarized norm measures and test overlaps hold. Parity and rotation symmetries of the original sequences pass to the profiles under the identifications of Lemma <a href="#lem:chart-compatibility" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:chart-compatibility">21</a>.
  - Explanation: Bounded sequences with locally bounded charge norms have subsequences with profiles on every proper stratum. The profiles carry exactly the limiting norm measure there and reproduce the test overlaps.
- **claim-collision-removal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-collision-removal)): Let $S_C\subset \mathcal Z_C$ be the union of the collision subspaces $c_\alpha=c_\beta$. If $F,G\in L^2(\mathcal Z_C)$, with the profile coefficient fiber, satisfy $D_u^C(m)F=G$ distributionally on $\mathcal Z_C\setminus S_C$, then the same identity holds on $\mathcal Z_C$. In particular, $F$ is in the full Euclidean graph domain. This remains true on the subspace with the permutation symmetry of the profile space.
  - Explanation: A distributional center-charge equation off the collision subspaces extends across them. The collision sets have codimension nine and carry no boundary condition.
- **claim-profile-liminf** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-profile-liminf)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:profile-compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:profile-compactness">24</a>, suppose in addition that $\left\lVert D_u(h_j,m)\psi_j\right\rVert$ is globally bounded. On a subsequence realizing its lower limit, the profiles satisfy $$\label{eq:profile-liminf} \sum_{C\text{ proper}}\left\lVert D_u^C(m)F_C\right\rVert^2 \le\liminf_{j\to\infty}\left\lVert D_u(h_j,m)\psi_j\right\rVert^2.$$ The derivatives on the left are the full-space graph derivatives.
  - Explanation: The sum of squared center-charge norms of the proper profiles is bounded above by the lower limit of the squared charge norms of the sequence.
- **claim-full-exhaustion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prof-full-exhaustion)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:profile-compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:profile-compactness">24</a>, after Proposition <a href="#prop:exterior-estimate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:exterior-estimate">22</a> has been proved at size $N$, there is also a full-collapse profile $F_0\in\mathcal K_N$. Its squared norm is the mass of the limiting norm measure at zero. If the squared norm measures are tight, then $$\label{prof:full-norm-exhaustion} \lim_j\left\lVert \psi_j\right\rVert^2 =\left\lVert F_0\right\rVert^2+\sum_{C\text{ proper}}\left\lVert F_C\right\rVert^2.$$ For any finite simultaneously extracted list, one likewise has $$\label{prof:polarized-exhaustion} \lim_j\left\langle \psi_j^a,\psi_j^b\right\rangle =\left\langle F_0^a,F_0^b\right\rangle+ \sum_{C\text{ proper}}\left\langle F_C^a,F_C^b\right\rangle.$$ The space $\mathcal K_N$ is allowed to have unknown dimension in this statement.
  - Explanation: A full-collapse profile in the kernel at size N exists. For tight sequences the norm and inner products split exactly into the full-collapse part and the proper profile parts, with no dimension assumption on the kernel.
- **claim-massive-tightness** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-massive-tightness)): There are $a>0$, $h_0>0$, and $R_0<\infty$, depending on $N$, such that $$\label{count:annular-confinement} \mathfrak q_{h,1}(\psi)\ge aR^2\left\lVert \psi\right\rVert^2 \quad\text{if}\quad h\ge h_0,\quad R\ge R_0,\quad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\psi\subset\{R<\|x\|<4R\}.$$ This inequality is on gauge invariants, with no rotation restriction. Consequently, for $R\ge R_0$, after enlarging the constants if needed, $$\label{count:tail-bound} \int_{\|x\|>4R}\|\psi\|^2 \le\frac{C}{R^2}\mathfrak q_{h,1}(\psi) +\frac{C}{R^4}\left\lVert \psi\right\rVert^2, \qquad h\ge h_0.$$ In particular, every bounded sequence of gauge- and $G$-invariant vectors with $h\to\infty$ and bounded $D_u(h,1)$ graph norms has tight squared norm measures.
  - Explanation: At large coupling and mass one, states supported on large annuli have form energy bounded below by the squared radius times the squared norm. This gives a tail bound and tightness of the norm measures.
- **claim-center-confinement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-count-center-confinement)): Fix a center space $\mathcal Z_C$, an arbitrary spectator Hilbert space $\mathcal E$, and a compact set $K\subset\{c\in\mathcal Z_C:\|c\|\ge b_0\}$, where $b_0>0$. Let $(v_\nu)_{\nu=1}^{16}$ be an orthonormal real spin basis and $m_j>0$ with $m_j\to\infty$. For each $\nu$, let $$a_{\nu,j}\in L^2(\mathcal Z_C;\mathcal F_C\otimes\mathcal E)$$ be supported in $K$, bounded in norm, and in the graph domain of the center charge $D_{v_\nu}^C(m_j)$, acting trivially on $\mathcal E$. Suppose that $$\left\lVert a_{\nu,j}-a_{1,j}\right\rVert\longrightarrow0, \qquad \left\lVert D_{v_\nu}^C(m_j)a_{\nu,j}\right\rVert=o(m_j) \quad(\nu=1,\ldots,16).$$ Then $\left\lVert a_{\nu,j}\right\rVert\to0$ for every $\nu$. No gauge or rotation invariance is imposed on these center states.
  - Explanation: Averaging over the spin basis cancels the angular terms, so nearby center states supported away from zero with small charge at growing mass must vanish in norm.
- **claim-large-coupling-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-large-coupling-gap)): On the gauge- and $G$-invariant Hilbert space, there are $\delta>0$ and $h_1<\infty$ such that, for all $h\ge h_1$, $$\label{count:odd-gap} \left\lVert D_u(h,1)\psi\right\rVert\ge\delta\left\lVert \psi\right\rVert \qquad(\psi\text{ odd}).$$ If $P_h$ is the orthogonal projection onto $\mathop{\mathrm{ker}}D_u(h,1)$ on that space, then $$\label{count:full-gap} \left\lVert (1-P_h)\psi\right\rVert\le\delta^{-1}\left\lVert D_u(h,1)\psi\right\rVert.$$ Moreover, the massive kernel is entirely even and has dimension $p(N)$.
  - Explanation: At large coupling the massive charge has a uniform gap on odd states, hence a gap on the complement of its kernel. The kernel is entirely even and has dimension equal to the partition number.
- **claim-proper-recovery** (construction, [source](#stmt-count-proper-recovery)): There are even gauge- and $G$-invariant vectors $v_{C,h}$, one for each proper partition, such that, as $h\to\infty$, $$\label{count:proper-recovery-estimates} \left\lVert D_u(h,1)v_{C,h}\right\rVert\longrightarrow0, \qquad \left\langle v_{C,h},v_{C',h}\right\rangle\longrightarrow\mathbf1_{C=C'}.$$ These vectors can be chosen simultaneously with recovery vectors for any prescribed finite list in $\mathcal K_N$, with all cross inner products tending to zero.
  - Explanation: Even invariant trial vectors, one per proper partition, are asymptotically orthonormal with vanishing charge, and can be chosen together with recovery vectors for any finite list in the kernel.
- **claim-whole-recovery** (construction, [source](#stmt-count-whole-recovery)): Let $\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_M$ be any finite orthonormal list in $\mathcal K_N$. There are even gauge- and $G$-invariant vectors $z_{a,h}$ such that $$\label{count:whole-recovery-estimates} \left\lVert D_u(h,1)z_{a,h}\right\rVert\longrightarrow0, \qquad \left\langle z_{a,h},z_{b,h}\right\rangle\longrightarrow\mathbf1_{a=b}.$$ They concentrate at zero in physical coordinates. No weighted moment condition on any $\phi_a$ is required.
  - Explanation: Any finite orthonormal list in the undeformed kernel is recovered by rescaled and cut-off vectors with vanishing massive charge, without any moment condition.

```json
        "exact_quote": "For fixed $h,m,v$, the charge $D_v(h,m)$ is essentially self-adjoint on the full smooth compactly supported core. Its closed domain is its distributional maximal domain $$\\{\\psi\\in L^2:D_v(h,m)\\psi\\in L^2\\text{ in distributions}\\}.$$ The same statement holds after restriction to any commuting compact group of gauge or rotation symmetries. On gauge invariants at $m=0$, all unit-spinor charges have the same graph domain, and $$\\left\\lVert D_v(h,0)\\psi\\right\\rVert^2=\\mathcal H(h,0)[\\psi].$$ Consequently the distributional $L^2$ kernel of any one of these charges is their common kernel. At $h=1$ it is exactly $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ker}}H_N$ for the form Hamiltonian in <a href=\"#eq:model-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:model-form\">[eq:model-form]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-270.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-normal-oscillators",
        "exact_quote": "The quadratic form $U_q^{(2)}$ is positive definite on $S_q$.         "anchor": "stmt-mass-slice-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $h_n\\downarrow0$ and $\\psi_n$ are gauge-invariant vectors of bounded norm and bounded $\\mathcal H(h_n,1)$ form energy. After a subsequence, there is one profile $f_q\\in L^2(S_q)\\otimes\\mathcal F_N$ for each of the $p(N)$ zero orbits, with the following properties. Each profile is $H_q$-equivariant and belongs to the oscillator form domain. With the normalizations described in the proof, $$\\label{mass:slice-exhaustion}\n \\lim_{n\\to\\infty}\\left\\lVert \\psi_n\\right\\rVert^2\n   =\\sum_q\\left\\lVert f_q\\right\\rVert^2,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_q H_q^{\\mathrm{nor}}[f_q]\n   \\leq\\liminf_{n\\to\\infty}\\mathcal H(h_n,1)[\\psi_n],$$ whenever the subsequence realizes the norm limit and energy liminf. For any fixed finite family of such sequences, extraction can be simultaneous and the norm identity polarizes to their inner products. Parity is preserved.",

        "exact_quote": "For every zero orbit there is a family $\\Phi_{q,h}\\in\\mathcal D$ of even gauge- and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spin}}(3)\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spin}}(6)$-invariant vectors such that, as $h\\downarrow0$, $$\\label{mass:vacuum-recovery-eq}\n \\left\\langle \\Phi_{q,h},\\Phi_{q',h}\\right\\rangle\\longrightarrow\\delta_{qq'},\n \\qquad \\left\\lVert D_h\\Phi_{q,h}\\right\\rVert\\longrightarrow0.$$",

          "anchor": "stmt-mass-vacuum-recovery",
          "exact_quote": "For every zero orbit there is a family $\\Phi_{q,h}\\in\\mathcal D$ of even gauge- and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spin}}(3)\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spin}}(6)$-invariant vectors such that, as $h\\downarrow0$, $$\\label{mass:vacuum-recovery-eq}\n \\left\\langle \\Phi_{q,h},\\Phi_{q',h}\\right\\rangle\\longrightarrow\\delta_{qq'},\n \\qquad \\left\\lVert D_h\\Phi_{q,h}\\right\\rVert\\longrightarrow0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-270.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slice",
        "exact_quote": "In a neighborhood of the chosen configuration, gauge-equivariant functions are equivalent to $K_0$-equivariant functions on $$x=b+z,\\qquad b\\in\\mathfrak k_0^9,\n \\qquad z\\in e^\\perp\\otimes\\mathfrak n.$$ The block spectra of $b_e$ lie in fixed disjoint windows. Up to a fixed positive orbit-volume constant, the density on the slice is $$j(b)=|\\det_{\\mathfrak n}K_e(b)|,\n \\qquad K_e(b)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{ad}}(b_e)|_{\\mathfrak n}.\n \\label{fast:density}$$ It is independent of $z$. The exact normalization is as follows. Give $\\mathop{\\mathrm{U}}(N)/K_0$ the invariant volume induced by the trace metric on the off-block color space, let $V_C$ be its total volume, and use normalized Haar measure when integrating the gauge orbit. With fixed labeled spectral windows the integrated slice measure is $V_Cj(b)\\,\\mathrm db\\,\\mathrm dz$. Thus, with $d_f=8\\dim\\mathfrak n$, the unitary flat-measure pullback is $$\\psi(b,t)=V_C^{1/2}j(b)^{1/2}h^{-d_f/4}\n                         \\Psi(b+t/\\sqrt h).\n \\label{fast:unitary-normalization}$$ Here the original field is restricted to the slice on the right. A fixed labeled chart has no additional permutation multiplicity. When all labels are used, division by the number of permutations of equal-sized blocks gives the quotient measure used in Definition <a href=\"#def:profile-space\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:profile-space\">19</a>. The same positive normalization is used in the inverse pullback for every test state. In these coordinates, $$D_v(h,m)=\\sqrt h\\,E_v(b)+D_v^s(h,m)+R_v(h,m).\n \\label{eq:slice-charge}$$ Here $D_v^s$ is precisely the charge on $\\mathfrak k_0^9$, including all its $h[b_i,b_j]$ multipliers. The operator $E_v(b)$ is an odd linear oscillator in $(p_t,t)$ using only fast fermions, with smooth coefficients independent of $h,m$. On a smaller chart the raw estimates imply $$\\|h^{-1/2}p_b\\psi_h\\|+\\|p_t\\psi_h\\|+\\|t\\psi_h\\|\\leq C.\n \\label{fast:chart-raw}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-270.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fast-line",
        "exact_quote": "Let $c$ range in a compact set of scalar block configurations with distinct centers and with the chosen $e$-separations bounded below. On the neutral space, $E_v(c)$ has a one-dimensional even kernel, independent of $v$, and a gap bounded below uniformly on this compact set and on unit spinors $v$. For $b$ sufficiently close to that set, including noncommuting $b$, it has an even rank-one kernel projection $P_v(b)$ and a uniformly bounded inverse on $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ran}}(1-P_v(b))$.*\n\n*Let $\\mathcal W^s$ be the oscillator Sobolev spaces defined below. For every prescribed finite integer $L$, the neighborhood can be chosen so that the parameter derivatives of $P_v$ and a normalized section $\\chi_v(b)$ through order $L$ have bounded oscillator moments through order $L$. The reduced inverse, extended by zero on the kernel, and its parameter derivatives through order $L$ are uniformly bounded as maps $\\mathcal W^s\\to\\mathcal W^{s+1}$ for $0\\leq s\\leq L$. The section can be chosen $K_0$-equivariantly so that its Berry form vanishes at $b=c$ in every slow direction. At scalar centers these sections are compatible with block permutations and the $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spin}}(3)\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spin}}(6)$ action, with no additional scalar character.",
        "source_id": "PM-270.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-test-lifts",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $m\\geq0$, a unit spinor $v$, and a partition $C$ with $k\\geq2$. For every $f\\in\\mathcal T_C$ there are gauge-singlet graph-domain states $I_{h,v,m}f$, defined for all sufficiently large $h$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|I_{h,v,m}f-\\mathcal J_hf\\|&\\longrightarrow0,\\notag\\\\\n \\|D_v(h,m)I_{h,v,m}f-\n                  \\mathcal J_h(D_v^C(m)f)\\|&\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{lift:graph-convergence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The construction is linear on any fixed finite-dimensional space of tests, and both conclusions hold simultaneously for every fixed finite test family. In particular, all inner products of their leading states and indicated charge outputs converge to the corresponding profile inner products. For distinct partitions the families, including their outputs, are asymptotically orthogonal. The leading identifications intertwine block permutations and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spin}}(3)\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spin}}(6)$; each fixed symmetry may be passed through these norm limits. The lifts may depend on $v$ and $m$.",
        "source_id": "PM-270.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

In particular, all inner products of their leading states and indicated charge outputs converge to the corresponding profile inner products. For distinct partitions the families, including their outputs, are asymptotically orthogonal.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-chart-compatibility",
        "exact_quote": "On overlaps of separated-center charts, the identifications $\\mathcal J_h$ agree to $o(1)$ in norm for each fixed test, with the natural center and internal fiber transports. A change of separating axis shifts the block variable $b$ by $O(|z|^2)$; its leading fast coordinate change is projection along the central conjugation directions, with the positive unitary density factor.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-intermediate-scales",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose <a href=\"#eq:exterior-estimate\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-estimate\">[eq:exterior-estimate]</a> holds at size $n$. Let $F_h$ have the gauge symmetry of that block, with $\\left\\lVert F_h\\right\\rVert\\le B$ and $\\left\\lVert D_u(h,0)F_h\\right\\rVert\\le K$. Both norms may include arbitrary spectator variables or a spectator Hilbert space. There is an absolute cutoff constant $C$ such that, whenever $L\\ge1$ and $M>e^LR_n$, $$\\label{prof:intermediate-bound}\n \\left(\\int_{Mh^{-1/3}<r<\\varepsilon}|F_h|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le c_n^{-1}\\left(e^L\\varepsilon K+\\frac{CB}{L}\\right).$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:intermediate-scales}\n \\lim_{\\substack{\\varepsilon\\downarrow0\\\\M\\to\\infty}}\n \\limsup_{h\\to\\infty}\n \\int_{Mh^{-1/3}<r<\\varepsilon}|F_h|^2=0.$$ The outer limit is directed: after choosing an error tolerance, one may choose $M_0$ and $\\varepsilon_0$ so that the bound holds for every $M\\ge M_0$ and $0<\\varepsilon\\le\\varepsilon_0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-profile-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the induction hypotheses at all sizes less than $N$. Fix $m\\ge0$, let $h_j\\to\\infty$, and suppose that $\\psi_j$ is a bounded sequence of gauge-invariant vectors with locally bounded $D_u(h_j,m)$ graph norms. Here local boundedness means that $\\left\\lVert D_u(h_j,m)(a\\psi_j)\\right\\rVert$ is bounded for every smooth compactly supported gauge-invariant multiplier $a$. After a subsequence there is a profile $F_C\\in\\mathcal P_C$ on each proper stratum such that the limiting norm measure restricted to that stratum is exactly its squared norm density. Moreover, for every test $f\\in\\mathcal T_C$ of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:test-lifts\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:test-lifts\">20</a>, $$\\label{prof:test-overlap}\n \\left\\langle \\psi_j,I_{h_j,u,m}f\\right\\rangle\\longrightarrow\\left\\langle F_C,f\\right\\rangle.$$ The extraction can be made simultaneously for any finite list of sequences, and then the analogous restrictions of all their polarized norm measures and test overlaps hold. Parity and rotation symmetries of the original sequences pass to the profiles under the identifications of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:chart-compatibility\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:chart-compatibility\">21</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-270.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $S_C\\subset \\mathcal Z_C$ be the union of the collision subspaces $c_\\alpha=c_\\beta$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prof-full-exhaustion",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:profile-compactness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:profile-compactness\">24</a>, after Proposition <a href=\"#prop:exterior-estimate\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:exterior-estimate\">22</a> has been proved at size $N$, there is also a full-collapse profile $F_0\\in\\mathcal K_N$. Its squared norm is the mass of the limiting norm measure at zero. If the squared norm measures are tight, then $$\\label{prof:full-norm-exhaustion}\n \\lim_j\\left\\lVert \\psi_j\\right\\rVert^2\n       =\\left\\lVert F_0\\right\\rVert^2+\\sum_{C\\text{ proper}}\\left\\lVert F_C\\right\\rVert^2.$$ For any finite simultaneously extracted list, one likewise has $$\\label{prof:polarized-exhaustion}\n \\lim_j\\left\\langle \\psi_j^a,\\psi_j^b\\right\\rangle\n =\\left\\langle F_0^a,F_0^b\\right\\rangle+\n       \\sum_{C\\text{ proper}}\\left\\langle F_C^a,F_C^b\\right\\rangle.$$ The space $\\mathcal K_N$ is allowed to have unknown dimension in this statement.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-massive-tightness",
        "exact_quote": "There are $a>0$, $h_0>0$, and $R_0<\\infty$, depending on $N$, such that $$\\label{count:annular-confinement}\n \\mathfrak q_{h,1}(\\psi)\\ge aR^2\\left\\lVert \\psi\\right\\rVert^2\n \\quad\\text{if}\\quad\n h\\ge h_0,\\quad R\\ge R_0,\\quad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\psi\\subset\\{R<|x|<4R\\}.$$ This inequality is on gauge invariants, with no rotation restriction. Consequently, for $R\\ge R_0$, after enlarging the constants if needed, $$\\label{count:tail-bound}\n \\int_{|x|>4R}|\\psi|^2\n \\le\\frac{C}{R^2}\\mathfrak q_{h,1}(\\psi)\n             +\\frac{C}{R^4}\\left\\lVert \\psi\\right\\rVert^2,\n \\qquad h\\ge h_0.$$ In particular, every bounded sequence of gauge- and $G$-invariant vectors with $h\\to\\infty$ and bounded $D_u(h,1)$ graph norms has tight squared norm measures.",
        "source_id": "PM-270.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-270.1"
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **profile space** ([source](#stmt-def-profile-space)): For an unordered partition $C=(n_1,\ldots,n_k)$ of $N$, with $k\geq2$, let its labeled center space be $$\mathcal Z_C=\left\{(c_1,\ldots,c_k)\in(\mathbb R^9)^k: \sum_\alpha n_\alpha c_\alpha=0\right\}, \qquad \|\mathrm dc\|^2=\sum_\alpha n_\alpha\|\mathrm dc_\alpha\|^2.$$
- **kernel spaces K_n** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Write $\mathcal K_n=\mathop{\mathrm{ker}}H_n$ and set $\mathcal K_1=\mathbb C$.
- **cluster profile** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A *cluster profile* is a wavefunction of these relative centers, with their fermions and with values in $\bigotimes_\alpha\mathcal K_{n_\alpha}$.
- **gauge dimension notation** ([source](#sec:model)): Fix an integer $N\ge2$ and put $d_N=N^2-1$.
- **undeformed Hamiltonian** ([source](#sec:model)): Let $H_N$ be the nonnegative self-adjoint operator associated with the closure of the quadratic form $$q_N(\Psi)=\frac1{16}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{16}\left\lVert Q_\alpha\Psi\right\rVert^2 \label{eq:model-form}$$ on the gauge-invariant compactly supported smooth core.
- **partition number** ([source](#stmt-prop-massive-index)): where $p(N)$ is the number of unordered integer partitions of $N$.
- **squared norm measure** ([source](#pm-h-24)): The squared norm measure of a vector $\psi$ is the pushforward under $\pi$ of $\|\psi(x)\|^2\,\mathrm dx$.
- **center space dimension** ([source](#pm-h-24)): of dimension $d_C=9(k-1)$.
- **classical zero set** ([source](#pm-h-14)): The zero set $Z=\{U_{1,1}=0\}$ is a finite union of compact gauge orbits.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-kernel-dimension | dimension of the zero-energy kernel | = | $1$ | every N at least two |
| q-massive-index | graded Fredholm index of the massive charge | = | $p(N)$ | every positive coupling |
| q-zero-orbit-count | number of classical zero orbits | = | $p(N)$ | matrix size N |
| q-proper-partition-count | number of proper partition types | = | $p(N)-1$ | partitions with at least two parts |
| q-coercivity-factor | coercivity factor of one charge against the averaged energy | ≥ | $\frac23$ | all couplings and masses |
| q-center-space-dimension | dimension of the labeled center space | = | $9(k-1)$ | k at least two |
| q-hardy-lower-bound | Hardy lower bound coefficient on the center space | ≥ | $\frac7{2\sqrt2}$ | center dimension at least nine |
| q-annulus-liminf | liminf of the weighted charge on unit vectors in a fixed annulus | ≥ | $\frac{5}{2\sqrt2}$ | coupling tending to infinity |
| q-exterior-constant | exterior estimate constant at size N | = | $1/\sqrt2$ | the threshold radius may depend on N |
| q-collision-capacity | squared gradient norm of the collision cutoff | ≤ | $C_K\varepsilon^7$ | small cutoff radius |
| q-boson-ground-energy | bosonic ground energy per color spin-l summand | = | $16l^2+16l+6$ | each color spin-l summand |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-square** [claim](#stmt-prop-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-square): Gamma-matrix trace identities and the canonical anticommutation relations give the kinetic, bracket, mass and rotation terms of the squared charge. -> exact square formula for the deformed charge
- **step-coercivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-coercive-charge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coercive-charge): Average the charge squares over the rotation group of the chosen spinor so the angular term cancels, then use positivity. -> coercive bound on invariant vectors
- **step-domains** [claim](#stmt-lem-domains) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-domains): Radial cutoffs and the symbol identity show that minimal and maximal domains coincide. -> closed domains and common kernel
- **step-rotation** [claim](#stmt-alg-rotation-exact) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-rotation-exact): Orthogonality of gamma monomials yields the orbital and spin terms of the anticommutator. -> exact rotation anticommutator
- **step-singlet-parity** [claim](#stmt-prop-singlet-parity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-singlet-parity): Pair the anticommutator with smooth kernel vectors using radial cutoffs and let the cutoff tail tend to zero. -> kernel vectors are Spin(9)-invariant and even
- **step-massive-potential** [claim](#stmt-lem-massive-potential) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-massive-potential): Contradiction by blow-up: a limiting direction would be a vanishing commuting configuration, using block traces. -> quadratic growth of the massive potential
- **step-common-domain** [claim](#stmt-mass-common-domain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mass-common-domain): Absorb the linear fermion term into the confining potential, then use Rellich compactness and the resolvent identity. -> common graph domain, compact resolvent, constant index
- **step-normal-oscillators** [claim](#stmt-lem-normal-oscillators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-normal-oscillators): Spin-coupling rules give the bosonic and fermionic frequencies; a determinant-character computation shows the ground line is trivial under the stabilizer. -> unique even invariant ground line
- **step-distance-confinement** [claim](#stmt-mass-distance-confinement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mass-distance-confinement): Combine positive normal Hessians near the orbits, a positive minimum on compact sets and the growth at infinity. -> distance-squared lower bound
- **step-slice-compactness** [claim](#stmt-mass-slice-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mass-slice-compactness): Normal tube charts, Rellich compactness on slices and a localization formula for the energy. -> profiles per zero orbit exhausting the norm
- **step-vacuum-recovery** [claim](#stmt-mass-vacuum-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mass-vacuum-recovery): Cut off the normal ground vector, transport it into the tube by gauge equivariance, and choose the cutoff radius diagonally. -> asymptotically orthonormal null vectors
- **step-massive-index** [claim](#stmt-prop-massive-index) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mass-vacuum-recovery-2): Odd gap by contradiction, even-odd pairing, upper bound from slice extraction, lower bound from recovery, constancy of the index. -> index equals the partition number
- **step-raw-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-raw-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-raw-estimates): Use the undeformed square identity with the fermion term bounded on compact sets. -> gradient and commutator estimates
- **step-exact-slice** [claim](#stmt-lem-slice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slice): Spectral windows give the slice; the Jacobian of conjugation gives the density; the charge is expanded in fast, slow and remainder parts. -> slice charge decomposition
- **step-fast-line** [claim](#stmt-lem-fast-line) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fast-line): Explicit oscillator at scalar centers, perturbation by resolvent Neumann series, and a Spin(9)-equivariant phase section. -> even rank-one fast projection with gap
- **step-fast-projection** [claim](#stmt-lem-fast-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fast-projection): Cut off the fast variables, square the cut-off charge, and use anticommutation with the slow charge and the fast gap. -> concentration on the fast line with slow graph bound
- **step-diagonal-compression** [claim](#stmt-lem-diagonal-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-diagonal-compression): Vanishing Berry term, parity arguments, and exact cancellation of orbit-spin and density terms. -> slow charge as the compression
- **step-chart-compatibility** [claim](#stmt-lem-chart-compatibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chart-compatibility): Implicit function theorem for the change of slice, then norm convergence by dominated convergence and approximation. -> chart-independent leading identifications
- **step-test-lifts** [claim](#stmt-prop-test-lifts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-test-lifts): Scale internal kernel vectors, correct by the reduced fast inverse to cancel the perpendicular output, then glue charts by a partition of unity. -> corrected lifts with convergent charge output
- **step-intermediate-scales** [claim](#stmt-lem-intermediate-scales) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-intermediate-scales): Logarithmic cutoff with slowly varying transition and the exterior estimate at the smaller size. -> quantitative exclusion of intermediate scales
- **step-profile-compactness** [claim](#stmt-prop-profile-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-profile-compactness): Remove the fast section, rescale internal variables, extract by Rellich compactness, and identify the stratum norm with the intermediate-scale estimate. -> profiles on proper strata
- **step-collision-removal** [claim](#stmt-lem-collision-removal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-collision-removal): Cutoffs near codimension-nine collision subspaces have small capacity. -> collision-free equation extends to the whole center space
- **step-profile-liminf** [claim](#stmt-prop-profile-liminf) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-profile-liminf): Pair the charge output with corrected test lifts, use Cauchy-Schwarz and Riesz representation, then remove collisions. -> liminf inequality for center charges
- **step-exterior-estimate** [claim](#stmt-prop-exterior-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-profile-liminf-2): Hardy inequality on center spaces of dimension at least nine on a fixed annulus, dilation, and weighted localization on logarithmic shells. -> exterior estimate at the current size
- **step-full-exhaustion** [claim](#stmt-prof-full-exhaustion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prof-full-exhaustion): Rescale near the origin, extract a local limit by compactness, identify its norm using the intermediate-scale estimate, and add the stratum contributions by tightness. -> norm and inner product exhaustion
- **step-center-confinement** [claim](#stmt-count-center-confinement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-count-center-confinement): Average the abelian square over the spin basis so that angular terms cancel, leaving a confining position term. -> vanishing norm of nearby center states away from zero
- **step-massive-tightness** [claim](#stmt-prop-massive-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-count-center-confinement-2): Contradiction after dilation, split by mass regime, fast projections and center confinement, then a squared radial partition for the tail bound. -> annular confinement and tightness
- **step-large-coupling-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-large-coupling-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-large-coupling-gap): Contradiction with a sequence of odd null vectors: profiles are even, so they vanish; then even-odd pairing gives the full gap. -> odd gap, full gap and kernel dimension
- **step-proper-recovery** [claim](#stmt-count-proper-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-count-proper-recovery): Cut off the profile vector, lift the tests, impose group and parity averaging, and choose thresholds diagonally. -> vectors recovering proper profile lines
- **step-whole-recovery** [claim](#stmt-count-whole-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-count-whole-recovery): Unitary dilation of kernel vectors with a fixed cutoff near zero, using concentration at the origin. -> vectors recovering any finite whole-block list
- **step-main-induction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-count-whole-recovery-2): Simultaneous induction: extraction of profiles gives non-triviality of the kernel; projecting recovery vectors into the massive kernel bounds the dimension by one. -> dimension of the kernel equals one

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-unique-kernel**: For every matrix size N at least 2, the relative undeformed SU(N) Hamiltonian has a kernel of dimension exactly one, and this kernel is Spin(9)-invariant and even.
- **inv-singlet-even**: Every gauge-invariant square-integrable vector in the undeformed kernel is a Spin(9) singlet with even fermion parity, with no finite-dimensionality or moment assumption.
- **inv-massive-index**: For every positive coupling the massive charge restricted to the invariant space is Fredholm with graded index equal to the number of integer partitions of N.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-gapped-continuum**: Treat the zero-energy state as lying below a gapped continuum and rely on a spectral gap in numerical or perturbative methods. -> The zero-energy state sits at the threshold of the continuous spectrum, so gap-based error bounds and isolated-eigenvalue arguments do not apply.
- **anti-fixed-massive-count**: Apply the unique-kernel count to a mass-deformed model at fixed positive coupling. -> The massive model has many classical vacuum orbits and a massive kernel of dimension given by the partition number, not one.
- **anti-nonsinglet-search**: Search for zero-energy states in non-singlet or odd-parity sectors. -> Every kernel vector is a Spin(9) singlet and even, so such sectors contain no zero-energy states and the search is wasted.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence, uniqueness and dimension statements proved by functional-analytic compactness, induction over matrix size and limiting arguments for an operator on an infinite-dimensional Hilbert space; the source gives no finite procedure with explicit steps that computes the kernel, so no finite reference implementation or oracle can be built.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an existence and uniqueness statement for a quantum-mechanical kernel and specifies no software component or interface. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns the zero-energy kernel of a supersymmetric matrix Hamiltonian, not a simulation scheme, and gives no time stepping or sampling rule. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool or checker is built; the theorem only restricts which symmetry sectors can carry a zero-energy state. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is a non-constructive compactness and induction argument; no finite procedure, complexity or error bound is given. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper introduces profile spaces and slices as analytic Hilbert-space objects, not as storable or indexable structures. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the result concerns circuits, parallel hardware or arithmetic precision. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or security notion appears in the paper. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, loss function or generalization statement; the analysis is purely spectral. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The quadratic form is studied for its kernel, not minimized by an algorithm, and no optimization guarantee follows. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, consensus or fault model appears in the paper. |
| other | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The result is an adaptable reference fact for mathematical physics and numerical spectral work on the undeformed relative SU(N) model: the zero-energy space is one dimensional, rotation invariant and even, which can prune symmetry sectors. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-size-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The kernel theorem is stated for the relative SU(N) model with the center of mass removed and with N at least two. It says nothing about models that keep the free center of mass or about N equal to one. Blocked use: ['Applying the unique-kernel count to a U(N) model with a free center-of-mass coordinate', 'Applying the count at matrix size one']
- **limit-threshold-no-gap** (edge_case): The zero-energy state is a threshold state: the continuous spectrum starts at zero, so there is no spectral gap below the continuum to exploit. Blocked use: ['Using gap-based perturbation or error bounds for the ground state', 'Treating the state as an isolated eigenvalue in a numerical spectral solver']
- **limit-operator-realization** (formal_scope): The statement concerns the specific self-adjoint realization given by the closure of the gauge-invariant form on the compactly supported smooth core. A different boundary realization is a different operator. Blocked use: ['Transferring the kernel count to realizations with prescribed boundary trace on valleys']
- **limit-massive-not-undeformed** (counterexample_scope): The massive deformation has a kernel of dimension equal to the partition number and several classical vacuum orbits, so the dimension-one count belongs only to the undeformed operator. Blocked use: ['Applying the dimension-one count to the mass-deformed charge at fixed positive coupling']
- **limit-induction-simultaneous** (hypothesis): The exterior estimate and the kernel dimension are proved together by induction on matrix size. The size-N step uses the one-dimensionality of the kernels at all smaller sizes and does not presuppose finite dimensionality at size N. Blocked use: ['Using the exterior estimate at size N independently of the smaller-size results', 'Citing local profile lemmas without the induction hypotheses']
- **limit-nonconstructive-extraction** (nonconstructive): Profiles and the kernel vector are obtained by compactness and subsequence extraction, with no explicit procedure, rate or computable bound for the extraction. Blocked use: ['Reading the proof as an algorithm for computing the kernel vector', 'Deriving runtime or accuracy guarantees for a solver']
- **limit-invariant-subspace-coercivity** (hypothesis): The coercive bound for a single charge holds only on gauge-invariant and torus-invariant vectors, so estimates derived from it do not apply to general vectors without that symmetry. Blocked use: ['Using the factor 2/3 bound on non-invariant vectors']
- **limit-hardy-dimension** (edge_case): The Hardy step works because each proper center space has dimension at least nine; it is used only on proper strata of the commuting locus. Blocked use: ['Using the Hardy argument where the center dimension is below the stated threshold']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every finite <i>N</i> ≥ 2, we prove that undeformed $`\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}\nolimits (N)`$ BFSS matrix quantum mechanics has exactly one normalizable zero-energy state after removing the center of mass. This establishes the threshold-bound-state conjecture.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Supersymmetric matrix quantum mechanics appears in the regularization of supermembranes (Wit, Hoppe, and Nicolai 1988, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[DeWitHoppeNicolai1988](#ref-DeWitHoppeNicolai1988)] and in the matrix-theory conjecture of Banks, Fischler, Shenker, and Susskind (Banks et al. 1997) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BFSS1997](#ref-BFSS1997)]. The model studied here describes nine Hermitian matrices and their fermionic partners. Its commuting configurations form noncompact flat directions. The D0-brane bound-state prediction of Witten (Witten 1996, sec. 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Witten1996BoundStates](#ref-Witten1996BoundStates)], subsequently central to matrix theory, is that after removing the free center of mass every finite matrix size has exactly one normalizable state of zero energy. We prove this threshold-bound-state conjecture for the $\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(N)$ model on its full configuration space. In the matrix-theory interpretation, this relative bound state supplies the internal state of a single supergraviton carrying $N$ units of longitudinal momentum (Banks et al. 1997, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BFSS1997](#ref-BFSS1997)]. The continuous spectrum starts at zero (Wit, Lüscher, and Nicolai 1989) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[DeWitLuscherNicolai1989](#ref-DeWitLuscherNicolai1989)], so the proposed eigenstate lies at its threshold rather than below a gapped continuum.

<a id="sec:model"></a>
#### The operator and the result

<a id="eq:model-charge"></a>
<a id="eq:model-form"></a>
Fix an integer $N\ge2$ and put $d_N=N^2-1$. Choose a traceless Hermitian basis $T_A$, $1\le A\le d_N$, with $$\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(T_AT_B)=\delta_{AB},\qquad
T_AT_B-T_BT_A=\mathrm if_{ABC}T_C.$$ Repeated indices are summed unless stated otherwise. The bosonic coordinates and momenta are $$X_i=x_i^AT_A,\qquad p_i^A=-\mathrm i\frac{\partial}{\partial x_i^A},
\qquad 1\le i\le9.$$ Let $\mathcal F_N$ be an irreducible complex Clifford module with self-adjoint generators $\theta_\alpha^A$, $1\le\alpha\le16$, satisfying $$\{\theta_\alpha^A,\theta_\beta^B\}
  =\delta_{\alpha\beta}\delta_{AB}.$$ Choose real symmetric $16\times16$ matrices $\gamma^i$ with $\{\gamma^i,\gamma^j\}=2\delta_{ij}\mathrm{Id}$, and set $\gamma^{ij}=[\gamma^i,\gamma^j]/2$, where the bracket in this formula is the ordinary matrix commutator. The sixteen supercharges on smooth compactly supported functions are $$Q_\alpha
 =p_i^A\gamma^i_{\alpha\beta}\theta_\beta^A
 +\frac12 f_{ABC}x_i^Bx_j^C
       \gamma^{ij}_{\alpha\beta}\theta_\beta^A.
\label{eq:model-charge}$$ The physical Hilbert space is $$\mathscr H_N
 =\bigl[L^2(\mathbb R^{9d_N})\otimes\mathcal F_N\bigr]^{\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(N)},$$ with the simultaneous adjoint action on bosons and fermions. Let $H_N$ be the nonnegative self-adjoint operator associated with the closure of the quadratic form $$q_N(\Psi)=\frac1{16}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{16}\left\lVert Q_\alpha\Psi\right\rVert^2
\label{eq:model-form}$$ on the gauge-invariant compactly supported smooth core. This fixes the coupling and the operator domain used throughout the paper. We use the exterior grading for the polarization $T=\gamma^1\gamma^2\gamma^3$, declaring its empty vector even and choosing that vector to minimize $\mathrm i\theta^tT\theta/2$. Section <a href="#sec:algebra" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:algebra">2</a> describes the associated symmetry actions.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every integer $N\ge2$, $$\dim\mathop{\mathrm{ker}}H_N=1.$$ The kernel consists of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(9)$-invariant states and is even in the fermion grading just specified.*

</div>

All square integrability in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> is on the whole space $\mathbb R^{9d_N}$. Constants in the proof may depend on the fixed integer $N$. The free $\mathop{\mathrm{U}}(1)$ center-of-mass coordinates do not occur in $\mathscr H_N$.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### History and the analytic issue

The supersymmetric index counts even and odd zero modes with opposite signs (Witten 1982) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Witten1982Index](#ref-Witten1982Index)]. In the present noncompact problem, contributions from infinity are essential. Yi (Yi 1997) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Yi1997](#ref-Yi1997)] computed the $\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(2)$ bulk term and proposed a free-particle argument for its boundary correction; Sethi–Stern (Sethi and Stern 1998) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[SethiStern1998](#ref-SethiStern1998)] obtained the two-particle index by including its boundary term. Moore–Nekrasov–Shatashvili (Moore, Nekrasov, and Shatashvili 2000) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[MNS2000](#ref-MNS2000)] computed the matrix integrals entering the principal contribution. Green–Gutperle (Green and Gutperle 1998, sec. 5) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[GreenGutperle1998](#ref-GreenGutperle1998)] argued that corrections from free identical clusters account for the divisor sum, assuming the free-cluster boundary prescription extends to general $N$. Konechny (Konechny 1998, sec. 3 and 5) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Konechny1999](#ref-Konechny1999)] derived a free leading asymptotic Hamiltonian and used it to conclude that the full $\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(N)$ index equals one. His expansion assumes that the eigenvalue differences of one diagonalized matrix grow at the same order. Our proof will control also the degenerating and nested cluster regions through explicit norm estimates.

A signed count becomes a dimension count once odd normalizable ground states are excluded. Sethi–Stern (Sethi and Stern 2000, sec. 3.2 and 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[SethiStern2000](#ref-SethiStern2000)] proved rotational invariance of normalizable ground states of ten-dimensional reductions. Hasler–Hoppe (Hasler and Hoppe 2002b, Theorem 1(a) and Lemma 2(c)) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[HaslerHoppe2002](#ref-HaslerHoppe2002)] gave a direct rotation anticommutator and cutoff proof on the full physical Hilbert space. In the fermion representation used here, singletness implies even parity. Section <a href="#sec:algebra" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:algebra">2</a> reproduces these arguments in our normalization and fixes the closed operator domains. The recent lectures of Lin (H. W. Lin 2026, sec. 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Lin2026v3](#ref-Lin2026v3)] describe the finite-$N$ spectral picture and distinguish the two-particle uniqueness consequence from the general conjecture.

Asymptotic wavefunction analysis supplies a second important line of work. Halpern–Schwartz (Halpern and Schwartz 1998) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[HalpernSchwartz1998](#ref-HalpernSchwartz1998)] developed a generalized Born–Oppenheimer search for $\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(2)$ candidates. Graf–Hoppe (Graf and Hoppe 1998) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[GrafHoppe1998](#ref-GrafHoppe1998)] computed the leading and subleading $\mathop{\mathrm{SU}}(2)$ asymptotics, and Fröhlich–Graf–Hasler–Hoppe–Yau (Fröhlich et al. 2000, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[FroehlichEtAl2000](#ref-FroehlichEtAl2000)] classified the corresponding invariant formal supercharge expansions at infinity. For general rank, Hasler–Hoppe (Hasler and Hoppe 2002a, 2–5) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[HaslerHoppeFactorization2002](#ref-HaslerHoppeFactorization2002)] conjugated the charge by the slice density, projected onto its transverse oscillator ground state, and obtained the leading free Cartan charge. Their cancellation of the gauge-spin and density terms is a direct predecessor of the computation in Section <a href="#fast:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fast:section">4</a>. Lin–Yin (Y.-H. Lin and Yin 2015, sec. 3.2 and 4) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[LinYin2015](#ref-LinYin2015)] developed a recursive cluster-factorization proposal and its first inverse-oscillator correction. These asymptotic constructions motivate the local objects used below; a global normalizable state additionally requires control of every cluster scale and of all limiting norm.

Mass deformation offers a finite count from which to approach that global problem. Porrati–Rozenberg (Porrati and Rozenberg 1998) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[PorratiRozenberg1998](#ref-PorratiRozenberg1998)] formulated a deformation criterion with explicit normalizability requirements on weighted representatives. Kac–Smilga (Kac and Smilga 2000) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[KacSmilga2000](#ref-KacSmilga2000)] counted mass-deformed vacua and identified persistence of normalizability at zero mass as the remaining hypothesis. We use the completely confining plane-wave deformation of Berenstein–Maldacena–Nastase (Berenstein, Maldacena, and Nastase 2002) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[BMN2002](#ref-BMN2002)]. Its classical zero orbits are indexed by integer partitions of $N$. Dasgupta–Sheikh-Jabbari–Van Raamsdonk (Dasgupta, Sheikh-Jabbari, and Van Raamsdonk 2002) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[DasguptaSheikhJabbariVanRaamsdonk2002](#ref-DasguptaSheikhJabbariVanRaamsdonk2002)] computed the quadratic fluctuation spectra around the reducible as well as irreducible vacua. Section <a href="#sec:massive-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:massive-index">3</a> rederives these frequencies and proves the stabilizer, domain, and norm-exhaustion statements needed for the index of our specified invariant charge. Chang’s refined BMN index (Chang 2025, sec. 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Chang2025](#ref-Chang2025)] also records signed contributions from states with nonzero angular momentum. The count below is the Fredholm index of a real charge on a specified invariant subspace.

The operator domain must remain fixed throughout this comparison. Boulton–García del Moral–Restuccia (Boulton, García del Moral, and Restuccia 2021, secs. 5–6) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Boulton2021](#ref-Boulton2021)] establish existence and uniqueness for a valley boundary-value problem with admissible prescribed trace. The realization with zero boundary trace has trivial kernel, while the solutions with prescribed nonzero trace need not be killed by the supercharges. That problem therefore concerns a different realization from <a href="#eq:model-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:model-form">[eq:model-form]</a>.

The task addressed here is the passage from a massive Fredholm count to the complete $L^2$ kernel of the undeformed full-space operator. The local oscillator projections and fast-inverse corrections are proved with graph-norm bounds near arbitrary proper clusters. An exterior estimate, established simultaneously with the kernel statement, excludes norm at intermediate internal scales. Together with massive confinement, it accounts for every limiting norm and inner product. This is the information that permits the final unsigned dimension comparison.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The proof and its compactness mechanism

Write $\mathcal K_n=\mathop{\mathrm{ker}}H_n$ and set $\mathcal K_1=\mathbb C$. The proof compares $\mathcal K_N$ with a massive problem whose index is the partition number $p(N)$, the number of unordered integer partitions of $N$. Two different uses of partitions enter this comparison. At small coupling, they label the massive classical vacuum orbits. In the limiting cluster decomposition, a proper partition $C=(n_1,\ldots,n_k)$, $k\ge2$, specifies the sizes of separated commuting blocks. Their distinct centers $c_\alpha\in\mathbb R^9$ satisfy $\sum_\alpha n_\alpha c_\alpha=0$. A *cluster profile* is a wavefunction of these relative centers, with their fermions and with values in $\bigotimes_\alpha\mathcal K_{n_\alpha}$.

For the dimension comparison, we study profiles of normalized massive states whose charge norms tend to zero; we call these null profiles. The proper partitions will contribute $p(N)-1$ lines of null profiles, while a full-collapse profile takes values in $\mathcal K_N$. The comparison uses norm-preserving extraction for existence, and recovery of prescribed profiles together with a massive spectral gap for uniqueness.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### The massive count.

We write $D_v(h,m)$ for the deformed charge paired with a real unit spinor $v$, where $h>0$ multiplies the commutator term and $m\ge0$ multiplies the linear mass term. Thus $D_v(1,0)=\sum_\alpha v_\alpha Q_\alpha$. Section <a href="#sec:algebra" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:algebra">2</a> chooses a spinor $u$ and a connected fixing torus $G$ so that, on gauge and $G$ invariants, $$D_u(h,m)^2\ge\frac23\mathcal H(h,m),$$ where $\mathcal H$ is the averaged squared charge. At fixed positive mass, the restricted charge is Fredholm. Section <a href="#sec:massive-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:massive-index">3</a> computes its index as $p(N)$ in the limit $h\downarrow0$ and proves constancy for all $h>0$.

We then study $h\to\infty$ at mass one. The unitary change of variables $y=h^{1/3}x$ sends $D_v(h,1)$ to $h^{1/3}D_v(1,h^{-2/3})$, so this limit is a massless limit at the original coupling. Internal block states shrink on the physical scale $h^{-1/3}$, while their relative centers remain on scale one.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Local profiles and their equations.

Near distinct block centers, a spectral slice separates the block variables $b$ from the off-block variables. After rescaling the latter, Section <a href="#fast:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fast:section">4</a> gives the exact decomposition $$D_v(h,m)=\sqrt h\,E_v(b)+D_v^s(h,m)+R_v.$$ Here $E_v$ acts on the transverse variables and their fermions, $D_v^s$ is the charge of the block variables, and $R_v$ collects the remaining slice terms. On the fast space fixed by the block-center gauge torus, $E_v$ has an even one-dimensional kernel and a local oscillator gap. A first-order estimate then forces bounded graph-norm sequences onto this line. In the scalar-block limit, compressing the charge onto this line gives the ordinary slow charge: the gauge-spin term cancels the derivative of the slice density.

To pass the charge equation to a profile, we need more than concentration of the state. Section <a href="#lift:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lift:section">5</a> constructs test states whose charge outputs converge in norm. A correction obtained from the reduced fast inverse cancels the perpendicular charge output of the leading oscillator product. Duality then identifies the center equation of each profile. Fixed physical cutoffs and $L^2$ concentration make these constructions available without moment bounds on the internal kernels.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Capturing every concentration scale.

Local profiles leave a possible loss of norm between the internal scale $h^{-1/3}$ and a vanishing physical radius. Section <a href="#sec:profiles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:profiles">6</a> excludes that loss by proving, simultaneously with Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the exterior estimate <a id="eq:intro-exterior"></a>$$\left\lVert rD_u(h,0)F\right\rVert\ge c_N\left\lVert F\right\rVert,
 \qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}F\subset\{h^{1/3}r>R_N\},\qquad r=|x|.
 \label{eq:intro-exterior}$$ It applies to compactly supported gauge-invariant vectors in the charge graph domain, without a rotation restriction. The smaller-block estimates make proper-cluster profiles account for all norm on a compact annulus. Their center spaces have dimensions $9(k-1)\ge9$, so the Euclidean Hardy inequality gives the exterior estimate at the current size. Only after this step do we extract the whole-block profile at the origin. The induction therefore uses no prior existence or dimension statement for $\mathcal K_N$.

Section <a href="#sec:counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:counting">7</a> proves a separate massive annular estimate that prevents escape to infinity. The resulting decomposition preserves all limiting norms and inner products. Each proper null profile has the unique even massive center-oscillator vacuum as its center factor. Induction on the internal blocks and the permutation symmetries then give one line for each of the $p(N)-1$ proper partitions. Every null profile is even: Proposition <a href="#prop:singlet-parity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:singlet-parity">6</a> proves this for $\mathcal K_N$ before its dimension is known. Consequently odd massive states have a uniform charge gap at large $h$. Spectral pairing gives the same gap on the even complement of the massive kernel, and the index becomes the exact massive dimension $p(N)$. Extracting these $p(N)$ directions forces a nonzero whole-block profile. Conversely, recovering any finite orthonormal whole-block list along with the $p(N)-1$ proper lines and projecting through the gap bounds that list by one. This closes the induction.

Figure <a href="#fig:induction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:induction">1</a> records this order of dependencies.

<figure id="fig:induction">

<figcaption>The simultaneous induction. Here $𝒦_{n} = ker H_{n}$ and $E_{n}$ is Proposition <a href="#prop:exterior-estimate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:exterior-estimate">22</a> at size $n$. The highlighted exterior step is completed before full collapse is used. Massive confinement follows independently from the local growing-mass estimates, and the index is computed independently at small coupling. At $N = 2$ all proper blocks are scalar, so the induction input is empty.</figcaption>
</figure>

```latex
\draw[flow] (local)--(proper);
\draw[flow] (local)--(tight);
\draw[flow] (tight.west)--++(-0.3,0)|-(exhaust.east);
\draw[flow] (parity)--(exhaust);
\draw[flow] (index)--(gap);
\end{tikzpicture}
```

<a id="sec:algebra"></a>
### The charge algebra, domains, and rotational invariance

We establish the operator framework needed for the index and limiting arguments: closed charge domains, a charge coercive on a chosen space of torus invariants, and even rotationally invariant undeformed kernel vectors.

We use the real Euclidean space $\mathfrak k$ of traceless Hermitian matrices, with scalar product $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(XY)$ and real Lie bracket $[X,Y]=(XY-YX)/\mathrm i$. Thus $\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}(X)$ is a real skew matrix on $\mathfrak k$. Uppercase Roman indices denote an orthonormal color basis and Greek indices denote spinor components; $i,j=1,\ldots,9$, $a,b,c=1,2,3$, and $d=4,\ldots,9$ are spatial indices. For $w\in\mathbb R^{16}$ and $z\in\mathfrak k$, write $\theta(w)\cdot z=\sum_{\alpha,A}\theta_\alpha^A w_\alpha z^A$. All Clifford tensor products are graded tensor products.

Set $$T=\gamma^1\gamma^2\gamma^3,\qquad
 s_a=2,\quad s_d=-1,
 \qquad A_i(v)=\gamma^i v,\quad
 B_{ij}(v)=-\gamma^{ij}v,\quad L_i(v)=s_i\gamma^iTv.$$ Here $T^t=-T$, $T^2=-\mathrm{Id}$, and $T$ commutes with the first three gamma matrices and anticommutes with the other six. For a unit real spinor $v$, coupling $h>0$, and mass $m\geq0$, define <a id="eq:deformed-charge"></a>$$\label{eq:deformed-charge}
\begin{aligned}
 D_v(h,m)&=\sum_i\theta(A_i(v))\cdot p_i
 +h\sum_{i<j}\theta(B_{ij}(v))\cdot[x_i,x_j]
 +m\sum_i\theta(L_i(v))\cdot x_i,\\
 \mathcal H(h,m)&=\frac1{16}\sum_{\nu=1}^{16}D_{v_\nu}(h,m)^2.
\end{aligned}$$ The second expression initially denotes a quadratic form on the gauge-invariant smooth compactly supported core; $(v_\nu)$ is any orthonormal real spin basis. Its closure will have the same notation.
