To see why there is only one stratum for each such partition, order its block sizes in a fixed way and attach a distinct center to each listed size. This labeled presentation is identified only under permutations of entries of equal size. Relabeling unequal sizes changes the presentation of the same commuting configuration; it does not define a new partition type. Thus there are exactly $p(N)-1$ proper types.

By the induction hypothesis, each internal kernel in <a href="#count:proper-profile-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="count:proper-profile-kernel">[count:proper-profile-kernel]</a> has dimension one. Choose the same normalized kernel vector whenever the block sizes agree. Those vectors are even. Exchanging two such factors consequently produces no graded sign. The center Gaussian is unchanged by the corresponding orthogonal permutation, and the empty center vacuum is fixed by its natural exterior action. Lemma <a href="#lem:fast-line" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:fast-line">16</a> supplies the same untwisted action for the fast section, with positive volume normalizations. Therefore <a href="#count:proper-profile-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="count:proper-profile-kernel">[count:proper-profile-kernel]</a> is exactly one dimensional for each proper partition. Let its normalized vector be $\Phi_C$.

<a id="stmt-count-proper-recovery"></a>
<div id="count:proper-recovery" class="lemma">

<a id="count:proper-recovery-estimates"></a>
**Lemma 31** (Recovery of the proper profile lines). *There are even gauge- and $G$-invariant vectors $v_{C,h}$, one for each proper partition, such that, as $h\to\infty$, $$\label{count:proper-recovery-estimates}
 \left\lVert D_u(h,1)v_{C,h}\right\rVert\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad
 \left\langle v_{C,h},v_{C',h}\right\rangle\longrightarrow\mathbf1_{C=C'}.$$ These vectors can be chosen simultaneously with recovery vectors for any prescribed finite list in $\mathcal K_N$, with all cross inner products tending to zero.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-count-proper-recovery"><summary>Proof of stmt-count-proper-recovery</summary>

*Proof.* We first approximate $\Phi_C$ in the center graph norm by admissible tests. Multiply its center factor by a smooth outer radial cutoff $\zeta_R$, equal to one for $|c|\le R$, zero for $|c|\ge2R$, and with derivative bounded by $C/R$. Multiply also by the collision cutoff $\rho_\varepsilon$ of Lemma <a href="#lem:collision-removal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:collision-removal">25</a>, chosen as a product over all unordered pairs. These cutoffs are rotation invariant and have the required permutation symmetry. The resulting vector $f_{C,R,\varepsilon}$ belongs to $\mathcal T_C$.

Since $D_u^C(1)\Phi_C=0$, only cutoff commutators contribute to its charge. The outer error tends to zero by the Gaussian tail. For fixed $R$, the smooth center factor is bounded on its support, so <a href="#prof:collision-capacity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="prof:collision-capacity">[prof:collision-capacity]</a> bounds the squared collision error by $C_R\varepsilon^7$. Its omitted $L^2$ norm also tends to zero, since the collisions have measure zero. Consequently there is a sequence of such tests $f_{C,\ell}$ with <a id="count:center-test-approximation"></a>$$\label{count:center-test-approximation}
 \left\lVert f_{C,\ell}-\Phi_C\right\rVert\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad \left\lVert D_u^C(1)f_{C,\ell}\right\rVert\longrightarrow0.$$ The order is explicit: for tolerance $1/\ell$, choose the outer radius first and then choose the collision radius small enough for that fixed outer support.

For each fixed $\ell$, Proposition <a href="#prop:test-lifts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:test-lifts">20</a> produces $w_{C,h,\ell}=I_{h,u,1}f_{C,\ell}$, with the prescribed leading norm and charge output. We impose the remaining symmetries carefully, since the chartwise corrections need not have been chosen rotation invariant. Let $A_G$ be Haar averaging over $G$, and let $E_+=(1+(-1)^F)/2$ be the even projection. For every fixed $g\in G$, Lemma <a href="#lem:chart-compatibility" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:chart-compatibility">21</a> and invariance of the profile give $$\left\langle w_{C,h,\ell},U_gw_{C,h,\ell}\right\rangle
                          \longrightarrow\left\lVert f_{C,\ell}\right\rVert^2.$$ These inner products are bounded uniformly in $g,h$ for fixed $\ell$. Haar dominated convergence and the projection identity $\left\lVert A_Gw\right\rVert^2=\left\langle w,A_Gw\right\rangle$ show $\left\lVert w_{C,h,\ell}-A_Gw_{C,h,\ell}\right\rVert\to0$. The identical argument for the two-element parity action gives $\left\lVert w_{C,h,\ell}-E_+A_Gw_{C,h,\ell}\right\rVert\to0$. Moreover, $D_u$ commutes with $A_G$ and interchanges the parity projections, so $$\left\lVert D_u(h,1)E_+A_Gw_{C,h,\ell}\right\rVert
                  \le\left\lVert D_u(h,1)w_{C,h,\ell}\right\rVert.$$ Thus imposing these symmetries preserves the leading norms and does not increase the charge errors.

For fixed $\ell$, the compact center supports on distinct partition strata are disjoint compact subsets of the gauge quotient. The leading identifications concentrate on those supports, so Lemma <a href="#lem:chart-compatibility" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:chart-compatibility">21</a> makes the different lifts asymptotically orthogonal. Their projections have the same property by the norm approximation just proved.

There are only finitely many proper partitions. Choose an increasing sequence $H_\ell\to\infty$ so that for every $h\ge H_\ell$ the finitely many norm, overlap, symmetry, and graph-output errors at the fixed test level $\ell$ are below $1/\ell$. Taking that level for $H_\ell\le h<H_{\ell+1}$, and normalizing the vectors, proves <a href="#count:proper-recovery-estimates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="count:proper-recovery-estimates">[count:proper-recovery-estimates]</a>. Additional finitely many cross overlaps can be included when choosing $H_\ell$; the required whole-block vectors and their concentration are constructed next. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-count-whole-recovery"></a>
<div id="count:whole-recovery" class="lemma">

**Lemma 32** (Recovery of any finite whole-block list). *Let $\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_M$ be any finite orthonormal list in $\mathcal K_N$. There are even gauge- and $G$-invariant vectors $z_{a,h}$ such that <a id="count:whole-recovery-estimates"></a>$$\label{count:whole-recovery-estimates}
 \left\lVert D_u(h,1)z_{a,h}\right\rVert\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad \left\langle z_{a,h},z_{b,h}\right\rangle\longrightarrow\mathbf1_{a=b}.$$ They concentrate at zero in physical coordinates. No weighted moment condition on any $\phi_a$ is required.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-count-whole-recovery"><summary>Proof of stmt-count-whole-recovery</summary>

*Proof.* Put $d=9(N^2-1)$, and let $$(S_h\phi)(x)=h^{d/6}\phi(h^{1/3}x).$$ Then $S_h$ is unitary and $D_u(h,0)S_h=h^{1/3}S_hD_u(1,0)$. Choose a fixed compact radial cutoff $\chi$ equal to one near zero, and set $z_{a,h}=\chi S_h\phi_a$. The vectors $S_h\phi_a$ concentrate at zero by their $L^2$ integrability: for every $\eta>0$, $$\int_{|x|>\eta}|S_h\phi_a|^2
                  =\int_{|y|>h^{1/3}\eta}|\phi_a(y)|^2\longrightarrow0.$$ It follows that $\left\lVert z_{a,h}-S_h\phi_a\right\rVert\to0$, proving the Gram matrix assertion. Lemma <a href="#lem:domains" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:domains">4</a> allows the cutoff product in the graph domain, and its undeformed charge is $[D_u,\chi]S_h\phi_a$. The derivative of $\chi$ is bounded and supported away from zero, so this norm tends to zero.

The massive multiplier has norm at most $C|x|$. On the fixed support of $\chi$, its norm on $z_{a,h}$ tends to zero as well: if the support is contained in $|x|\le B$, then $$\left\lVert |x|z_{a,h}\right\rVert^2
 \le\eta^2\left\lVert \phi_a\right\rVert^2+
                   B^2\int_{|x|>\eta}|S_h\phi_a|^2.$$ Take $h\to\infty$ and then $\eta\downarrow0$. This proves the charge estimate without assuming $|y|\phi_a\in L^2$. Proposition <a href="#prop:singlet-parity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:singlet-parity">6</a> makes every $\phi_a$ even and a rotation singlet; radial cutoff and dilation preserve these symmetries.

At any fixed level of the proper-partition construction, the compact center supports avoid collisions and hence avoid zero. Its lifts are therefore asymptotically orthogonal to all the $z_{a,h}$. There are only finitely many such pairs. Include these overlaps in the threshold selection in Lemma <a href="#count:proper-recovery" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="count:proper-recovery">31</a> to obtain the simultaneous claim there. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-32"></a>
#### Closing the simultaneous induction

<details id="proof-stmt-count-whole-recovery-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-count-whole-recovery</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* Assume that $\dim\mathcal K_n=1$ and Proposition <a href="#prop:exterior-estimate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:exterior-estimate">22</a> hold for $2\le n<N$. Section <a href="#sec:profiles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:profiles">6</a> first proves the exterior estimate at size $N$, using only proper profiles on compact annuli. It then constructs the full-collapse profile in $\mathcal K_N$. Proposition <a href="#prop:massive-tightness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:massive-tightness">28</a> and Lemma <a href="#lem:large-coupling-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:large-coupling-gap">30</a> now show that, for all sufficiently large $h$, the massive kernel on gauge and $G$ invariants has dimension exactly $p(N)$, with a uniform gap on its complement.

Choose any sequence $h_j\to\infty$ in this range and an orthonormal $p(N)$-element basis of its massive kernel for each $j$. Simultaneously extract profiles from this finite list. Equation <a href="#prof:polarized-exhaustion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="prof:polarized-exhaustion">[prof:polarized-exhaustion]</a> says that the resulting list is orthonormal in <a id="count:limit-target"></a>$$\label{count:limit-target}
              \mathcal K_N\ \oplus\!\!\bigoplus_{C\text{ proper}}\mathbb C\Phi_C.$$ The proper summand has dimension $p(N)-1$. If $\mathcal K_N$ were zero, this space could not contain the orthonormal list. Hence $\mathcal K_N\ne\{0\}$.

For the opposite inequality, take an arbitrary finite orthonormal list $\phi_1,\ldots,\phi_M$ in $\mathcal K_N$. Lemmas <a href="#count:proper-recovery" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="count:proper-recovery">31</a> and <a href="#count:whole-recovery" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="count:whole-recovery">32</a> provide $L=p(N)-1+M$ asymptotically orthonormal even gauge- and $G$-invariant vectors $w_{1,h},\ldots,w_{L,h}$ with charge norms tending to zero. The gap <a href="#count:full-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="count:full-gap">[count:full-gap]</a> implies $$\left\lVert w_{a,h}-P_hw_{a,h}\right\rVert\longrightarrow0
                  \qquad(1\le a\le L).$$ Their projected Gram matrix therefore tends to the $L$-by-$L$ identity. For all sufficiently large $h$ it is positive definite, so the $L$ projected vectors are linearly independent in the $p(N)$-dimensional massive kernel. Thus $$p(N)-1+M\le p(N),\qquad M\le1.$$ This applies to every finite orthonormal list, and hence proves $\dim\mathcal K_N\le1$ without presupposing finite dimensionality. Together with existence it gives $\dim\mathcal K_N=1$.

For $N=2$, every proper internal block has size one. Its kernel factor is $\mathbb C$, and there are no smaller nonabelian exterior estimates to assume. The same argument therefore starts the induction. At each subsequent size the order is: smaller-block kernel and exterior results, proper-stratum profiles, the current exterior estimate, the full-collapse profile, and finally the massive dimension comparison. This completes the simultaneous induction. Lemma <a href="#lem:domains" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:domains">4</a> identifies $\mathcal K_N$ with the kernel of the form Hamiltonian in <a href="#eq:model-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:model-form">[eq:model-form]</a>, proving Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> on the entire configuration space. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The simultaneous induction. Here $𝒦_{n} = ker H_{n}$ and $E_{n}$ is Proposition 22 at size $n$. The highlighted exterior step is completed before full collapse is used. Massive confinement follows independently from the local growing-mass estimates, and the index is computed independently at small coupling. At $N = 2$ all proper blocks are scalar, so the induction input is empty.",
      "figure_id": "fig:induction",
      "pdf_page": 6,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:induction",
          "exact_quote": "The simultaneous induction. Here $\\mathcal K_n=\\ker H_n$ and\n$E_n$ is Proposition~\\ref{prop:exterior-estimate} at size $n$.\nThe highlighted exterior step is completed before full collapse is\nused. Massive confinement follows independently f",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n  box/.style={draw=blue!55!black,rounded corners=2pt,align=center,\n    text width=7.5cm,inner sep=7pt,font=\\small},\n  side/.style={box,text width=4.8cm,draw=black!60,\n    inner sep=5pt,font=\\footnotesize},\n  flow/.style={-{Stealth[length=2mm]},thick,draw=black!70},\n  node distance=6mm and 9mm]\n\\node[box] (input) {Induction input for $2\\le n<N$:\\\\\n  $\\dim\\mathcal K_n=1$ and exterior estimate $E_n$};\n\\node[box,below=of input] (proper) {Proper-cluster profiles capture all norm\\\\\n  on compact annuli; corrected tests give center charges};\n\\node[box,below=of proper,fill=blue!7] (ext) {Hardy inequality in dimension $9(k-1)\\ge9$:\\\\\n  establish the current exterior estimate $E_N$};\n\\node[box,below=of ext] (collapse) {Capture the full-collapse profile in $\\mathcal K_N$\\\\\n  kernel nontriviality and dimension remain unknown};\n\\node[box,below=of collapse] (exhaust) {Exhaustion of norms and inner products;\\\\\n  every limiting null profile is even};\n\\node[box,below=of exhaust] (gap) {Odd gap and spectral pairing;\\\\\n  massive kernel has dimension $p(N)$};\n\\node[box,below=of gap] (count) {$p(N)-1$ proper profile lines;\\\\\n  extraction gives existence;\\\\recovery and the gap give uniqueness};\n\\node[box,below=of count,fill=blue!7] (output) {Induction output: $E_N$ and $\\dim\\mathcal K_N=1$};\n\\node[side,right=of proper] (local) {Local fast analysis and\\\\corrected test lifts\\\\\n  (Sections~\\ref{fast:section}--\\ref{lift:section})};\n\\node[side,right=of collapse] (tight) {Massive annular confinement\\\\\n  prevents escape to infinity\\\\(Proposition~\\ref{prop:massive-tightness})};\n\\node[side,right=of exhaust] (parity) {Current-size singlet and parity\\\\\n  (Proposition~\\ref{prop:singlet-parity}),\\\\$\\dim\\mathcal K_N$ unrestricted};\n\\node[side,right=of gap] (index) {Independent massive\\\\Fredholm index $p(N)$\\\\\n  (Proposition~\\ref{prop:massive-index})};\n\\foreach \\a/\\b in {input/proper,proper/ext,ext/collapse,collapse/exhaust,\n  exhaust/gap,gap/count,count/output}\\draw[flow] (\\a)--(\\b);\n\\draw[flow] (local)--(proper);\n\\draw[flow] (local)--(tight);\n\\draw[flow] (tight.west)--++(-0.3,0)|-(exhaust.east);\n\\draw[flow] (parity)--(exhaust);\n\\draw[flow] (index)--(gap);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Flowchart diagram illustrating the steps of the simultaneous induction proof, arranged into a primary vertical column of eight stacked boxes on the left and a secondary column of four boxes on the right.\n\nLeft column (main induction flow, connected sequentially by downward arrows):\n1. Top box (rounded rectangle, purple border):\n   \"Induction input for $2 \\le n < N$:\n   $\\dim K_n = 1$ and exterior estimate $E_n$\"\n   $\\downarrow$\n2. Box 2 (rectangle):\n   \"Proper-cluster profiles capture all norm\n   on compact annuli; corrected\n   tests give center charges\"\n   $\\downarrow$\n3. Box 3 (rectangle, shaded purple / highlighted):\n   \"Hardy inequality in dimension $9(k - 1) \\ge 9$:\n   establish the current exterior estimate $E_N$\"\n   $\\downarrow$\n4. Box 4 (rectangle):\n   \"Capture the full-collapse profile in $K_N$\n   kernel nontriviality and di-\n   mension remain unknown\"\n   $\\downarrow$\n5. Box 5 (rectangle):\n   \"Exhaustion of norms and inner products;\n   every limiting null profile is even\"\n   $\\downarrow$\n6. Box 6 (rectangle):\n   \"Odd gap and spectral pairing;\n   massive kernel has dimension $p(N)$\"\n   $\\downarrow$\n7. Box 7 (rectangle):\n   \"$p(N) - 1$ proper profile lines;\n   extraction gives existence;\n   recovery and the gap give uniqueness\"\n   $\\downarrow$\n8. Bottom box (rounded rectangle, purple border):\n   \"Induction output: $E_N$ and $\\dim K_N = 1$\"\n\nRight column (supporting inputs and intermediate results):\n- Box R1 (rectangle):\n  \"Local fast analysis and\n  corrected test lifts\n  (Sections 4–5)\"\n  - Has a directed arrow pointing left to Box 2 of the left column.\n  - Has a directed arrow pointing downward to Box R2.\n- Box R2 (rectangle):\n  \"Massive annular confinement\n  prevents escape to infinity\n  (Proposition 7.1)\"\n  - Has a directed arrow pointing left to Box 4 of the left column.\n- Box R3 (rectangle):\n  \"Current-size singlet and parity\n  (Proposition 2.5),\n  $\\dim K_N$ unrestricted\"\n  - Has a directed arrow pointing left to Box 5 of the left column.\n- Box R4 (rectangle):\n  \"Independent massive\n  Fredholm index $p(N)$\n  (Proposition 3.1)\"\n  - Has a directed arrow pointing left to Box 6 of the left column."
    }
